Native grammar and compiler lifetime qualification

P10d2b1 · 2026-10-11T17:17:59.287Z · v22.12.0 · linux/x64 · AMD Ryzen 7 5800H with Radeon Graphics. 3 alternating fresh processes per implementation and workload.

Complete original facts: equal across every run and both lifetimes
Shared-workload sampled tree peak: 651.0 / 603.7 MiB (-7.3%). Parser-child peak: 565.3 / 516.6 MiB. Total measured shared parsing: 2242 / 2166 ms (-3.4%).

The indexing pipeline first completes native grammar/query extraction and reaps every native worker generation. The separate compiler worker may prewarm its Node/helper bootstrap during native extraction; it imports TypeScript and parses qualified original literal JavaScript in ERB/Razor/Liquid only after every native generation exits. Both retain the ten-second per-job deadline, 256 MiB V8 old-space limit and original extraction budgets; native WASM/RSS is not capped by that V8 setting. Completed syntax-cache records contain both phases; a failed compiler cannot retain partial native-only output. Standalone parser calls keep their existing complete-source behavior.

Twenty native grammar profiles use recorded authored original fixture bytes. Shared runs reuse native grammars across the sequence, followed by one compiler phase. Isolated runs use fresh native/compiler processes per source profile. First-use times include process startup, integrity/query/grammar loading, original parsing and extraction; template times include the remaining compiler startup after bootstrap overlap. They are end-to-end latency observations, not isolated WASM compilation timings. Repeated calls validate complete fact equality and expose reuse costs.

A separate process samples all thread descendants every 25 ms with acknowledged start markers. The native phase may overlap a source-free compiler bootstrap. Before every compiler dispatch the harness asserts that all native generations have exited; sampled process counts remain in the raw data. This separates active native WASM assets from the active TypeScript compiler heap, while allowing lightweight startup to overlap. RSS sums can count shared pages repeatedly and miss short peaks; incomplete samples remain recorded. The isolated profile additionally measures the startup tradeoff; its total before/after is 9423 / 8467 ms. These thin parser workloads exclude repository graph/compiler services and history concurrency; full mixed and TS/Laravel comparisons qualify those separately. Broader WASM budgets, deeper histories and native operating systems remain open. The initial split-worker characterization and first bootstrap attempt preserve all observations from superseded implementations: both reduced sampled memory but failed latency guards. The unbundled grammar profile and thirty-run unbundled mixed profile also preserve every observation: Small cold indexing missed the guard (+11.5%). The final implementation uses a plain JavaScript service bundled from the same typed source. It prewarms only the source-free bootstrap, imports TypeScript inside a source job after native exit, and reaps failed generations before recovery. Build and conformance checks require the exact approved helper set, the job-local dynamic compiler import, and byte equality between the checked bundle and regenerated source. The compiler child loads no source TypeScript loader; the runtime compiler/version and original extraction budgets remain the same. Native and compiler jobs have separate ten-second deadlines; a qualified template can consume two active jobs, excluding queue waits. Standalone complete parser calls keep their existing single ten-second job. Final mixed graphs/history/tree-RSS comparison and TS/Laravel comparison qualify repository indexing separately. Only Codiluce’s own parsers/compiler service run; no target code, target dependency, target runtime, target compiler or render program executes.

Baseline: 055e6d2fde933f14a295888b251d569d72db5f48. Frozen implementation: 39a5be0d9687bc636e31eb28f35608893fec6a8964e53b996df7af5287b9747d (319 files).
